Headlining this release is a new ship model, a complete rebalance of all ships in the game, the ability to install multiple weapons per ship, many improvements to user interface, and more.\n\n### Natrix Reworked\n\nThe overhaul of our ship models has now come to the OPLI-Barnard Inc. manufactured Natrix line, with a beautiful new model and vastly improved capability for this not-so-little ship. While not intended for a combat role in the same way as the Coronatrix, the Natrix still has plenty of defensive firepower to ensure your deliveries go where they are needed in a timely fashion.\n\n![](https://bszlrd.hu/images/projects/2024-07-26-pioneer/natrix_station_02.png)\n\n![](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/90d8ebf2-9f4d-4a6f-a057-7eb317795e50)\n\n### User Interface\n\nWe have focused on improving a number of things about the user interface. Cargo is now fully separate from installed equipment; you won't have to choose between carrying cargo or equipping that beam laser any longer.\n\nWe've decided to make ship equipment slots first-class citizens rather than just a simplistic numeric limit, which has allowed us to finally add support for multiple weapons per ship rather than the hard-coded singular forward and rear weapon mount (if you happened to be rich enough to even afford a ship with a rear weapon mount!)\n\nThis has also enabled us to implement a much-needed improvement to the game's hyperdrives, converting them to sip fuel from an internal reservoir rather than chugging an entire cargo container of fuel from the cargo hold. Hyperspace jumps in small ships should take quite a bit less cargo space to carry out now. For those curious, the [hyperdrive design document](https://dev.pioneerspacesim.net/design-document/hyperdrive-redesign) outlines the thought that went into the new {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_vrtx_011Ch5GQi9UJw5Yz3Cc22AWC","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I fixed the station model data in `data/models/stations/new_ground/new_ground_landing.dae`; no C++ changes were needed. I couldn't watch the video: `/app/.harbor_assets/` is empty, so `recording.mp4` isn't in the workspace. I worked from the written description instead. I haven't launched the game or watched the hull readout through a landing, so the damage fix is untested in play.\n\n**Cause:** On the `new_ground` surface station, the invisible collision boxes for the landing pads were higher than the visible pad surface:\n\n| Pad collision box | Top of box | Visible surface and landing point |\n|---|---|---|\n| `collision_pad1` | 73.308 | 72.582 |\n| `collision_pad02` to `06` | 72.838 | 72.582 |\n\nThe game places a landed ship with its underside exactly at the landing point (72.582). So a ship resting on a pad already overlapped the pad's collision box: by 0.73 m on pad 1 and 0.26 m on the other five. That's the \"outlines overlap while sitting still\" symptom, and the overlap is what turns a clean landing into a collision that damages the hull. On the other ground station (`ground_station`), the pad boxes sit exactly at the landing-point height, which supports this reading.\n\n**Fix:**\n- All six pad boxes now have their tops at 72.58245, flush with the visible surface, so every pad on the station matches.\n- I renamed `collision_pad1` to `collision_pad01` to match the others.
